#7185. Senior Product Marketing Manager - Scaling Creators at Kit

Kit is the email marketing platform experts choose to grow their audience, deepen their relationships, and build a business around the work they love. For coaches, YouTubers, authors, podcasters, and other creatives, there isn't a better marketing hub to rely on to grow audiences, automate email marketing, and sell digital products — all within one platform.More importantly, there isn't a team more committed to helping creators earn a living. We're on a mission to help creators earn $1 billion using our creator marketing platform. We have always been 100% independent and 100% remote.We are proud to have built a product that our customers love, and we look for people who have enthusiasm and belief in our mission, vision, and values to join our team. We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

Kit's most established creators run real companies, with teams, large lists, dedicated sending needs, and a buying process that our self-serve motion wasn't built to serve. Because this part of our business is growing, we're looking for a Senior Product Marketing Manager, Scaling Creators to own how Kit wins, lands, and expands these businesses through a sales-assisted motion that sits alongside our self-serve product.

You'll define who this buyer is, write the story that resonates and wins with them, and build the GTM strategy to drive inbound pipeline for our sales team. When these creators choose Kit and grow on it, they build more valuable businesses, and that's the outcome this role exists for.

This is a full-time individual contributor role ideal for someone who has added an upmarket sales-led motion to a product-led company before and wants their work measured in win rate and deal size.

You'll report to Christina Lhi, Head of Marketing, and work alongside two other experienced Product Marketing Managers on Kit's product marketing team. Together, you'll keep the self-serve and sales-assisted stories feeling like one Kit.

Our sales leadership, on who we sell to, the deal toolkit, enablement, and win/loss

Account Executives and Solutions Consultants, on demo narratives, discovery, and technical and migration proof

Product, on packaging, launches, and roadmap input

RevOps and analytics, on how accounts get routed to sales, attribution, and pricing decisions

Creator Success, on case studies, customer references, and expansion plays

Demand gen, content, and events, on campaigns and events for established creators

First Week: Dive into our onboarding curriculum in Notion and learn Kit's product, pricing, and current positioning. Attend Get-To-Know-You (GTKY) meetings with marketing, sales, Solutions Consultants, RevOps, and Creator Success. Get access to our CRM, call recordings, and product analytics, and start listening to recent sales calls. Understand how product marketing currently works at Kit

First Month: Shadow 15+ sales calls and talk with 10 established creators, including recent wins, losses, and churned accounts. Audit the sales content reps use today and how product usage currently routes accounts to sales. Set baselines with RevOps for win rate, sales cycle, deal size, and expansion revenue. Share a written point of view with the entire growth team on who our established creator buyer is and the three biggest gaps in how we sell to them. And an action plan on how we can win these creators.

First Year: Sales runs on messaging and tools you built, pitch with confidence and we drive 2X inbound MQLs from when you started. You’ll own and lead our channel strategy, setting the vision, driving cross-functional alignment, and partnering with Marketing to bring it to life. You'll run a quarterly win/loss program and share what you learn openly, build a clear path for sales leads on our website, and make sure launches that matter to larger accounts ship with both a self-serve and a sales-assisted version. Your work has a direct impact on helping Kit reach $100,000,000 ARR while protecting the self-serve engine that got us here.

Segmentation and buyer definition: turning firmographic and product usage data into a clear picture of who to sell to and when

Messaging and positioning for a sales-assisted buyer, including competitive differentiation and objection handling

Sales enablement that reps actually use: demo narratives, discovery guides, business cases, and battlecards

Writing and storytelling that turns product depth into a business case a non-technical buyer can act on

Working with CRM, product analytics, and pipeline data to measure funnel conversion, win rate, and deal size

Customer and market/competitor research, including win/loss interviews and call analysis

Systems thinking: connecting people, processes, data, and tools to identify opportunities and build repeatable solutions across the go-to-market motion

Strong AI fluency: knowing when to leverage AI and how to build AI-powered workflows or tools that scale product marketing work, with human judgment and quality checks built in

Pricing and packaging input for a new tier or add-on (nice to have)

7+ years in B2B SaaS product marketing, including at least 2 years supporting a sales team selling to mid-market and/or larger accounts

Added a sales-led motion on top of a product-led one at a high-growth company, and can show what changed before and after with clear metrics

Built a deal toolkit and enablement program from little or nothing, and tied it to a sales metric like win rate or deal size

Ran a win/loss or customer research program that changed how a team sold or built

Ran an expansion or account-based program for an existing customer base (nice to have)

Worked in email, martech, or the creator economy

You lead without a formal title. You align sales, product, RevOps, and finance on goals, trade-offs, and what success looks like, then keep things moving.

You advocate for creators in decisions, and you protect the self-serve experience instead of pushing every account toward sales.

You act with urgency and stay focused on hitting OKRs and owning outcomes.

You work in public. You share findings, drafts, and the reasoning behind decisions so others can build on them.

You connect data to decisions, and you help others get sharper about using it.

You raise the bar on quality and iterate toward it, and you shape how your team uses AI, from call analysis to keeping battlecards current.

Experience in only self-serve or only enterprise sales. This role lives in the space between them, and you'll need to understand both.

Treating enablement as a deck handoff. The work includes coaching reps, measuring what gets used, and revising what doesn't land.

Wanting a sales playbook already in place. Much of this motion doesn't exist yet, and you'll be building it as you learn.

Preferring to be measured on output rather than outcomes. You'll share accountability with Sales for win rate, deal size, and expansion revenue.

Needing extensive approval before acting. You'll be trusted to make calls on speed versus quality and to explain your reasoning.

Discomfort with direct, candid feedback, especially from sellers on what's working in live deals.

We're 100% remote with team members across the globe. We work mostly on East Coast time—so if you're based on the West Coast of the United States, Europe, or Asia, you might have a few early mornings or late evenings. We do our best to keep things reasonable, but a little time zone juggling comes with the territory.

Kit has standardized salaries based on position, no matter where you live. For this role, we’re hiring at our level 4 ($166,000). Level is determined based on experience and our interview process.

We cover up to $2,280 per month toward medical premiums, with dental and vision premiums fully covered. We offer Health Insurance plans through Aetna

$2,000 equipment allowance for your first two years, $1,000 budget every following two years. Company-provided laptops are issued to every Kit team member and are not included in the equipment budget

Individual learning + development budget ($3,500/year)

Creator meetup budget ($50/month) to connect with creators over coffee, lunch, or other in-person meetups

Childcare benefit up to $3,000 annually through JOON

Twenty (20) days of paid time off during each year of employment

Paid paid vacation: An after-tax bonus of $1,000 for taking five consecutive days of vacation where you’re fully unplugged from work

Two weeks of paid sick time each year, including mental health + well being days

Twelve (12) weeks paid parental leave and flexible scheduling in your child’s first year

Up to six weeks of paid bereavement leave, medical leave, and disaster after six months of employment, two weeks of each paid leave in your first six months

Winter Break Closure: Kit closes for a week at the end of December, giving everyone a collective break to enjoy the holiday season. Essential support services remain available, with teams coordinating to ensure coverage during this period

Four-week, paid sabbatical after five years with the team

Fantastic in-person or virtual retreats with the team twice a year

We usually receive a large number of applications, and our goal is to follow up with you within two weeks. You’ll hear from us either way. Here’s what you can expect from the rest of our hiring process:

A phone screen with our recruiter (30 min)

We’ll keep you informed through every step. To get started, complete this application, including a cover letter and answers to the questions on the next page. We read every single application, and your cover letter and responses help put your experience into context.

We value diversity in all of its forms. Research tells us that applicants who are female or non-binary, as well as applicants who are people of color, are less likely to apply for roles they do not feel 100% qualified for. If you think you meet more than 50% of our requirements but fewer than 100% of them, please apply.

We’re imperfect communicators, so think of our job postings as the starting point for discussion rather than proof that you shouldn’t apply.

Kit does not discriminate on the basis of race, sex, color, religion, age, national origin, marital status, disability, veteran status, genetic information, sexual orientation, gender identity or any other reason prohibited by law in provision of employment opportunities and benefits.

New remote jobs delivered to your inbox, every week.

Location independence is the future of work. It serves companies with best talent from around the globe. And benefits employees with a greater work life balance and the ability to work in their pyjamas.- Nithur, founder.

Treating enablement as a deck handoff. The work includes coaching reps, measuring what gets used, and revising what doesn't land.

Wanting a sales playbook already in place. Much of this motion doesn't exist yet, and you'll be building it as you learn.

Preferring to be measured on output rather than outcomes. You'll share accountability with Sales for win rate, deal size, and expansion revenue.

Needing extensive approval before acting. You'll be trusted to make calls on speed versus quality and to explain your reasoning.

Discomfort with direct, candid feedback, especially from sellers on what's working in live deals.

We're 100% remote with team members across the globe. We work mostly on East Coast time—so if you're based on the West Coast of the United States, Europe, or Asia, you might have a few early mornings or late evenings. We do our best to keep things reasonable, but a little time zone juggling comes with the territory.

Kit has standardized salaries based on position, no matter where you live. For this role, we’re hiring at our level 4 ($166,000). Level is determined based on experience and our interview process.

We cover up to $2,280 per month toward medical premiums, with dental and vision premiums fully covered. We offer Health Insurance plans through Aetna

$2,000 equipment allowance for your first two years, $1,000 budget every following two years. Company-provided laptops are issued to every Kit team member and are not included in the equipment budget

Individual learning + development budget ($3,500/year)

Creator meetup budget ($50/month) to connect with creators over coffee, lunch, or other in-person meetups

Childcare benefit up to $3,000 annually through JOON

Twenty (20) days of paid time off during each year of employment

Paid paid vacation: An after-tax bonus of $1,000 for taking five consecutive days of vacation where you’re fully unplugged from work

Two weeks of paid sick time each year, including mental health + well being days

Twelve (12) weeks paid parental leave and flexible scheduling in your child’s first year

Up to six weeks of paid bereavement leave, medical leave, and disaster after six months of employment, two weeks of each paid leave in your first six months

Winter Break Closure: Kit closes for a week at the end of December, giving everyone a collective break to enjoy the holiday season. Essential support services remain available, with teams coordinating to ensure coverage during this period

Four-week, paid sabbatical after five years with the team

Fantastic in-person or virtual retreats with the team twice a year

#7150. AI Native Head Of Growth - EdTech (French fluent 🇫🇷) at Paradox

Most growth teams call themselves data-driven. They have a dashboard, an agency and a ChatGPT licence to write ad copy faster. That’s the 99%.

We want to be part of the 0.1%: a company where AI doesn’t just help the growth team, it runs the growth loop. Every day, on every channel.

Then it’s your chance to define a new category : The Operating System (OS) for Human Greatness Augmented with AI.

Science-backed. Design-led. AI-powered. Global.

We’re not looking for someone who only advises on what to test, we’re looking for someone who builds it. A hands-on Head of Growth who can ideate, write the copy, design the strategy and launch experiments directly.

1. Generate hypotheses. Read the signals (ad performance, webinar attendance, sales calls, objections, churn reasons, comments, competitors’ ads) and propose what to test next, with a cost and an expected impact.

2. Act across every tool. Not a chatbot in a tab: it reads and writes in our whole stack, from ad accounts to CRM, landing pages, webinars, email, social DMs and analytics.

3. Hold the full context of Paradox. Our DNA, offers, voice, member stories, past campaigns and what they taught us, in one shared company brain.

4. Ship its hypotheses. Produce the hook, the ad cut, the page, the email sequence or the funnel variant, and put it live.

5. Measure with real KPIs. Every test is tied to a number that matters (cost per qualified lead, show-up rate, close rate, CAC, payback, LTV), not to likes or reach.

6. Learn and loop on its own. Scale what works, kill what doesn’t, write the lesson back into the brain, and launch the next cycle without waiting for a meeting.

This is already real. Our growth hub tracks every hypothesis and experiment, with its result. An ads library of thousands of creatives feeds our creative research. Agents cut ads from long-form content, prepare webinars, wire Instagram comment-to-DM automations and audit our tracking after every live. Athena, our AI coach, works with members every day.

We believe self-help is broken: generic theory, a quick high, no real change.

We believe people deserve more. More than content. More than motivation.

They deserve a system that knows them, one that adapts, challenges, and grows with them.

Growth at Paradox means bringing people into something that actually changes their life, and proving it.

- Human Intelligence (coaches, peers, and mentors)

- Structured Intelligence (protocols, mental models, rituals)

- Technological Intelligence (AI personalization, feedback loops, adaptive journeys)

To create one seamless ecosystem: Paradox OS.

Paradox was founded by David Laroche, a creator followed by over 2 million people and known for his interviews with icons like Tony Parker, Mike Horn, Richard Branson and Gad Elmaleh. We started in French. We’re building for the world.

Here’s the bet: a company that learns by itself grows in a way a company of people alone can’t. A small growth team running a self-learning machine can out-test, out-learn and out-grow teams a hundred times its size.

We hold ourselves to the standards of Apple, Spotify and MasterClass. While others hide their numbers, we let our results speak.

As Head of Growth, you’ll be the architect of a growth machine that learns by itself. In a classic growth team, people do the work and AI helps. Here it’s the other way round: agents do the work, you design the system, set the bar and make the calls.

You don’t just manage a team, you get your hands dirty to identify growth levers and pull them yourself.

❌ The old way: a Head of Growth hires people and agencies, briefs them, and checks their work.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: your team is mostly agents, and you build them yourself. You write their skills, plug them into our tools (ad accounts, CRM, webinars, email, analytics) and write the evals that tell an agent what "good" means: a hook that stops the scroll, a page that converts, a test that’s worth its cost.

You give autonomy step by step: first an agent suggests, then it acts once you approve, then it acts alone within a budget and guardrails. You move each agent up a level only when its track record earns it.

Example: in your first month you build the agent that reviews every ad set overnight. At first it only suggests. By month three it pauses losers and moves budget by itself within limits you set, and you only see what was decided and why.

❌ The old way: a media buyer checks the dashboards each morning, changes bids by hand and waits for the weekly report.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: you build and improve the agents that watch every ad set, page, webinar and email sequence around the clock. They spot what’s dropping, find the cause, fix it or launch a variant, and report what they did.

You give autonomy step by step: an agent can pause an ad on its own long before it can raise a budget. You decide where each agent stops and a human takes over.

Example: show-up rate drops on Tuesday’s webinar. Before anyone notices, an agent has found the cause (a reminder that never went out), fixed it and started testing a new reminder sequence.

❌ The old way: a creative team briefs an agency, waits two weeks and gets five ads.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: you build the agents that turn every live, interview and member story into hooks, ad cuts and shorts, that study what the best advertisers in the world are running, and that write in our voice.

Your taste becomes the eval: you write down what makes a great hook, a great cut and a great story, in rules and examples the agents check themselves against. Each time you reject something, the reason goes back into the rules.

Example: one webinar becomes dozens of hook variants by the next morning. You keep the best few, and next week the agents’ first drafts are already closer to your bar.

❌ The old way: an analyst rebuilds the numbers in a spreadsheet at the end of the month, and nobody trusts them.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: you build the agents that connect every touch to cash, from ad to lead, live, sale and transformed member, and that audit the tracking after every campaign.

No number, no test: you define the KPIs that matter (CAC, show-up, close rate, LTV, payback), and agents refuse to launch a test that isn’t wired to one.

Example: after each live, an agent cross-checks the ad platform, the CRM and our analytics, finds where they disagree and fixes the leak before the next live.

❌ The old way: lessons live in people’s heads and leave with them.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: every result goes back into the company brain, and agents read it before proposing anything. The machine never learns the same lesson twice.

You measure the machine itself: tests per week, time from idea to live, the share of the work done without a human, and how many agents moved up an autonomy level this month. You push those numbers up.

And always: no fake scarcity, no fake claims, real stories told with consent. The machine can scale, but it doesn’t decide what’s right. You do.

How you’ll know you’re AI-native: you spend your time building agents, writing evals and making calls, not doing the work by hand. Most experiments are generated and shipped by agents. The output grows every month while the headcount doesn’t.

You’ll own these KPIs: LTV / CAC ratio, funnel conversion rate by stage, experiments launched per week.

Desired outcomes: conversion rates 2x better than baseline, growth experimentation that runs continuously, a predictable growth engine.

Tools you’ll master: GA4, Optibase, Vidalytics, Clarity, Hotjar, Amplitude, Figma, Looker Studio, VWO, Optimizely, Claude Code, and whatever helps you see what others miss.

You have ideally a minimum of 3 years of experience in Conversion Rate Optimization, Growth Experimentation, or Performance Marketing, ideally within SaaS, e-commerce, or digital product environments.

You have a track record of designing, implementing, and scaling A/B and multivariate tests that have generated measurable improvements in revenue, user activation, or conversion.

You’ve built with AI and agents yourself: Claude Code, automations, APIs, evals, creative pipelines. Show us something you built, not a certificate.

A Conversion Architect, skilled at diagnosing funnel bottlenecks and translating data into structured experimentation roadmaps that deliver measurable business outcomes.

A Behavioral Strategist, fascinated by what drives human action, able to mix cognitive psychology, UX, and persuasion frameworks to design experiences that convert because they resonate.

A Data-driven Creator, fluent in GA4, Hotjar, AB Tasty, VWO, Amplitude, not just running tests, but connecting insights to revenue, retention, and lifetime value.

Loop thinker: you see every channel as a loop that can learn, and you can’t stop yourself from closing it.

Taste: you know a great hook in two seconds, and you’d rather ship one great ad than ten average ones.

Numbers-honest: you say when a test failed. You care about real revenue, not vanity metrics.

Builder, not manager: you’d rather build the system than coordinate an agency.

Growth over ego: you love hard feedback, you read, study and learn fast.

Cross-disciplinary: you move from psychology to spreadsheet to creative brief to code.

Analytical: you turn a vague problem into a clear hypothesis, a metric and an experiment.

English: Fluent English, you can hold a conversation on specific details with English-speaking colleagues.

Our chosen language for written communication is English.

A Note on Recruiting Scams: Scammers sometimes pose as Airbnb recruiters to get money or personal information from candidates. A few things that will always be true for Airbnb’s hiring process: our open roles are posted on Airbnb’s Career’s Page at careers.airbnb.com, and our recruiters correspond only from @airbnb.com or @ext.airbnb.com email addresses. Our recruiters will never ask for your Social Security number, bank account details, passport, or payment app information while you are interviewing.  We’ll also never ask you to pay a fee, send money, deposit or cash a check, or purchase work-related equipment (such as a company laptop) during the interview process. We encourage candidates to remain vigilant of these recruiting scams and not share sensitive information if you do not believe an individual is actually affiliated with Airbnb.

#7146. Graduate Software Engineer, Open Source and Linux, Canonical Ubuntu at Canonical

We are hiring 2026 and 2027 Graduate Software Engineers into engineering teams around the world. As a global platform company we offer a wide range of software engineering challenges, in both open source products and commercial business systems. We have teams that work in a variety of languages, typically Python, Golang, Rust, C/C++, JavaScript and of course... Bash. We work from the very lowest levels of the Linux system - BIOS and boot loaders, firmware, kernel and drivers, all the way up to desktop applications and containers. We work on tiny devices like the Raspberry Pi and new RISC-V boards, all the way up to supercomputers, clouds and Kubernetes clusters. We work on compilers and toolchains, and we work on security, cryptography, performance and documentation. Some of our teams focus on packaging thousands of pieces of software, others focus on deep contributions to a single body of code.

If you are excited about the potential that open source has for humanity and the enterprise, you have outstanding results in your academic career, and you are excited to contribute to the open source stack, this is the best way to apply to Canonical. We will work with you to identify teams and projects that would be interesting and a good fit for your skills and motivations. The process is highly competitive, it will require effort and excellence to succeed if you apply here.

Most of our engineering teams work from home. We try to align a single team in a single time zone - EMEA (Europe, Middle East and Africa), APAC (Asia Pacific and Indian Ocean), and AMER (North, South and Central Americas). Even though you work from home, we try to ensure you always have colleagues alongside to coach and mentor you, and for the companionship of shared problem solving and pair programming. Larger projects have multiple teams and might span the globe. We work with the open source community and specialize in building teams that work well in the 'fishbowl' of open source engineering. This is full time work, five days a week, often solving hard problems with significant responsibility. We don't generally work weekends and nights and we do enjoy good holidays but we also expect productive and intense engagement Monday to Friday.

We bring all our engineering teams together in person twice a year for a deep 'sprint' with other teams to stimulate discussion and enable a wide range of career development paths. This is a fantastic way to demonstrate and develop your skill in software, learn about a wide range of technologies, make a meaningful impact to open source, and see some very interesting new cities that you might never otherwise get to visit.

If you find the open source stack amazing, and you have been a consistent high-performing student in your school and university, then this will be a challenging and exciting start to your career.

Location: This is a Globally remote role.

We consider geographical location, experience, and performance in shaping compensation worldwide. We assess performance and revisit compensation twice annually to ensure we recognize outstanding colleagues. In addition to base pay, we offer a performance-driven annual bonus or commission. We provide all team members with additional benefits which reflect our values and ideals. We balance our programs to meet local needs and ensure fairness globally.

Canonical is a pioneering tech firm at the forefront of the global move to open source. Our platform, Ubuntu, is very widely used in breakthrough enterprise initiatives such as public cloud, data science, AI, engineering innovation, and IoT. Our customers include the world's leading public cloud and silicon providers, and industry leaders in many sectors. We are founder-led, profitable, and growing.

Powered by worldwide distributed collaboration since our inception in 2004, we believe tech and business leadership can emerge anywhere. We have no central headquarters. Most of us work from home, but we are exceptionally disciplined about this. We have very high expectations of all colleagues, and are champions of excellence. Working here is a step into the future and will challenge you to think differently, work smarter, learn new skills, and raise your game.

We are proud to foster a workplace free from discrimination. Diversity of experience, perspectives, and background create a better work environment and better products. Whatever your identity, we will give your application fair consideration.

New remote jobs delivered to your inbox, every week.

Location independence is the future of work. It serves companies with best talent from around the globe. And benefits employees with a greater work life balance and the ability to work in their pyjamas.- Nithur, founder.

#7139. AI Native Creative Strategist - EdTech (French fluent 🇫🇷) at Paradox

What if your next playground was to build a global, iconic Brand, with a creative machine that never sleeps, and compounds?

We’re not looking for a "Social Media Manager" to follow a calendar. We’re looking for an AI-native Creative Strategist (Brand & Content).

Someone who understands that to win today, you don’t just need "content", you need a Movement.

This isn’t another content job. Most content teams use AI to write captions faster. That’s the 99%. We want to be in the 0.1%: a media where AI runs the creative loop, from the signal to the hook, the cut, the post and the lesson. Every day, on every platform, in French and in English. You bring the taste. The machine you build brings the scale.

Here’s the bet: a company that learns by itself grows in a way a company of people alone can’t. We aim for $10M in revenue with 10 people, each of them running a dozen agents.

Paradoxically, the more AI runs our content, the more human it has to feel. AI doesn’t make more of what everyone makes. It makes more of what only we can make.

1. Read the signals. Comments, DMs, retention graphs, saves, shares, search trends, objections from sales calls, and what the best creators and advertisers in the world are publishing right now.

2. Hold the full context of Paradox. Our DNA, years of interviews, lives and courses, member stories, and every past post with what it taught us, in one shared company brain.

3. Generate in our voice. Hooks, scripts, shorts, carousels, cuts, thumbnails and captions, in French and in English.

4. Ship. Format, schedule, publish and answer, on every platform.

5. Measure with real KPIs. Retention, shares, saves, follower growth, and the followers who become leads and members. Not likes.

6. Learn and loop on its own. Double down on what works, kill what doesn’t, write the lesson back into the brain, and start the next cycle without waiting for a meeting.

This is already real. An ads library of thousands of creatives feeds our creative research. Agents cut ads from long-form content and wire Instagram comment-to-DM automations. And our product is AI-native too: Athena, our AI coach, works with members every day.

At Paradox, we are convinced that greatness is trainable, and we believe self-help is broken.

Most learning platforms are used the same way: consuming generic theories, getting quick highs, ending with no real change.

People deserve more: a system that knows them, one that adapts, challenges, and grows with them.

That’s why we want to empower people at scale, by leveraging cutting edge technologies like AI, and crafting user experiences with outstanding aesthetics.

- Human Intelligence (coaches, peers, and mentors)

- Structured Intelligence (protocols, mental models, rituals)

- Technological Intelligence (AI personalization, feedback loops, adaptive journeys)

To create one seamless ecosystem: Paradox OS.

Paradox was founded by David Laroche, a creator followed by over 2 million people and known for his interviews with icons like Tony Parker, Mike Horn, Richard Branson and Gad Elmaleh. We started in French. We’re building for the world.

You lead the Content Factory not by management, but with creativity.

In a classic content team, people do the work and AI helps. Here it’s the other way round: agents do the volume, you design the machine, set the bar and make the calls. Humans do what no machine can: taste.

Build a symbiotic strategy where David Laroche’s personal brand and Paradox’s company brand reinforce each other without cannibalization.

Implement radical new formats (documentaries, conceptual shorts) that shatter the boring codes of the "Self-Help" industry.

Define distinct, sharp editorial lines for each platform: Depth & Search for YouTube, Aesthetic & Vibe for Instagram, and Authority & Authenticity for LinkedIn.

❌ The old way: a strategy deck written once a year, and a calendar filled by habit.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents watch every platform, every competitor and every comment continuously. They tell you which angle is rising, which format is tiring and which story our audience asks for. The strategy updates every week, on evidence.

Take responsibility for the entire production chain from hooks to storyboarding the narrative, and ensuring the final cut matches the vision.

Own the packaging: titles, thumbnails and the first 3 seconds. You test variants every week and know your click-through and retention numbers by heart.

❌ The old way: one shoot becomes one video and a handful of posts.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: one shoot, one live or one interview becomes dozens of hooks, shorts, carousels and ad cuts by the next morning, in both languages. Concepts are tested with AI-generated drafts before we spend a day shooting them.

Your taste becomes the eval. You write down what makes a great hook, a great cut, a great carousel and a great story, in rules and examples the agents check themselves against. Each time you reject something, the reason goes back into the rules.

Act as the final quality check: nothing gets published if it doesn’t meet the "Paradox Standard" of aesthetics and storytelling, whether a person or an agent made it.

❌ The old way: a creative team briefs an editor, waits three days and gets two versions.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents draft, cut, caption, translate and schedule. You keep the best, and next week their first drafts are already closer to your bar.

Analyze retention graphs, CTR, and CPL to iterate based on feedback, killing what fails and aggressively scaling what works.

❌ The old way: someone pulls the stats at the end of the month and nobody changes anything.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents read the numbers of every post every day, explain why it worked or didn’t, and propose the next test. You measure the machine itself: posts per week, time from idea to live, hit rate, and the share of the work done without a human.

We started in French. You build the machine that takes Paradox to English-speaking audiences: channels, formats and voice that feel native, not translated.

And always: real stories told with consent, no fake claims, no fake scarcity. The machine can scale, but it doesn’t decide what’s right. You do.

How you’ll know you’re AI-native: you spend your time on concepts, taste and calls, not on resizing, captioning and exporting. The output grows every month while the team doesn’t.

Proven Content Builder: You have successfully grown a media brand, a YouTube channel, or a personal brand to significant scale (100k+ followers or millions of views). You understand the mechanics of virality and retention. You’ve created at least one format that broke out, and you can show its click-through and retention numbers.

Elite Scripting Skills: You can show us scripts you wrote that performed. You know how to structure a narrative, write a hook that stops the scroll, and keep an audience engaged for 20+ minutes.

Performance Fluency: You have worked in environments where content had to drive business results (ads, launches, conversion), not just vanity metrics.

AI-native Creative: you’ve built content pipelines with AI yourself: Claude Code, automations, generative video, image and voice tools, evals. Show us something you built, not a certificate.

Intolerant to Mediocrity: You have "Taste." You are physically pained by a bad font, a clumsy cut, or a weak headline. You fight for the details that others ignore, including in what AI produces.

Psychology Obsessed: You don’t just "make content"; you study human nature. You constantly ask why people click, why they share, and what emotion drives them.

A "Doer" with Authority: You command respect not by title, but by competence. You can jump into a project, write the script, direct the shoot, and fix the edit if needed.

Loop thinker: you see every platform as a loop that can learn, and you can’t stop yourself from closing it.

No Ego, Just Impact: You don’t fall in love with your ideas. You accept that even the best concepts can flop. When it happens, you don’t take it personally; you analyze the data, pivot immediately, and ship the next concept with renewed energy.

English: Fluent English, you can hold a conversation on specific details with English-speaking colleagues.

Our chosen language for written communication is English.

❌ If you need clarity before chaos: wrong place.

❌ If you prefer balance over intensity: wrong season.

❌ If you don’t genuinely care about the topics we cover: wrong place.

❌ If you want to strategize without getting your hands dirty in scripting, directing, or the editing suite: wrong role.

❌ If you think AI content means generic content: wrong role. We use AI to multiply what only we can make.

❌ If you want to manage a large team: we’d rather build the machine than grow the headcount.

Help us market a product that fuses neuroscience, design, and AI to reinvent how humans grow.

Build one of the first truly AI-native media, and write the playbook others will copy.

Work with filmmakers, psychologists, engineers, and creatives obsessed with impact.

You’ll compete with San Francisco companies.

But you don’t just want a competitive paycheck, you also want…

-> Playing a crucial role in the development of a company whose content and products will be used by millions of people

-> 😎 A company culture based on autonomy & accountability

Our recruitment process is tailored to do our best to avoid that you or us realize that we’re not a perfect fit. Every step serves a purpose and will allow us to get to know you better as much as it’ll help you understand more where you’ll be stepping in.

We also want you to have time to ask all of your questions regarding the mission, the long-term vision of your team and the company, our values, with the relevant interlocutor in front of you.

At Talkspace, we are committed to fostering a diverse, equitable, inclusive, and belonging-centered workplace where everyone can thrive while making a difference in mental health. Want to help over two million people receive quality mental healthcare? Come join our mission of getting therapy in the hands of everyone!

Our team at Wisdo Health is seeking NBHWC-certified Health and Wellness Coaches to support adults participating in a virtual group coaching program designed to complement their weight management journey, including individuals using GLP-1 medications. Coaches lead weekly online group sessions that help members build sustainable habits around nutrition, physical activity, stress management, and long-term behavior change.

This is a remote position based in the United States Talkspace Headquarters is located in NYC.

At Talkspace, we believe that pay transparency during the interview process is a critical part of diversity, equity, and inclusion. Our salary bands are based on internal and external compensation benchmarks, which we regularly evaluate to ensure we pay competitively.

Talkspace is the world’s leading online therapy company, serving over 2 million users looking to begin their wellness journey through tele-health. According to the World Health Organization, close to 1 billion people worldwide live with a mental disorder, and on average more than 75% with mental, neurological, and substance use disorders receive no treatment for their condition at all. Additionally, one-third of the world’s population – 2 billion people – live in countries that spend less than 1% of their health budgets on mental health. Therapy is an universal need and it's our mission here to change the world by cultivating an intentional space for people to feel supported through quality care that is simple and accessible.

Combining our passion for innovation along with our desire to help others overcome the stigma behind “getting help,” we are transforming the way patients find the right care provider, making an otherwise impossible feat easily conquerable. Our network of licensed, accredited, and board-certified clinicians are increasing access to mental health for our members through a myriad of high quality therapy services: anytime and for a fraction of the price. Dedicated to our mission, we are looking for candidates that want to bring their talents into a diverse  “for purpose” space. If you’re equally as passionate about making quality mental healthcare accessible to all then Talkspace is the right place for you!

Talkspace welcomes and celebrates talent from all backgrounds, perspectives, and walks of life to foster an innovative and diverse workforce. We encourage you to apply, even if you don’t meet every qualification or if your path has been nontraditional — such as not completing a formal degree program, taking a career break, or having a prior criminal record — if you believe you could make a great addition to this team. Come as you are and learn about the exciting opportunities on our team.

Individuals seeking employment at Talkspace are considered without regard to race, color, religious creed, sex, national origin, citizenship status, age, physical or mental disability, sexual orientation, marital, parental, veteran or military status, unfavorable military discharge, or any other status protected by applicable federal, state or local law.

How do we define Diversity, Equity, Inclusion, and Belonging at Talkspace?

Diversity encompasses the unique attributes of our employees as individuals. We value and embrace the richness arising from their varied backgrounds, perspectives, and experiences, which include, but are not limited to, age, ability, ethnicity, gender, race, and cultural background.

Equity refers to a fair and impartial workplace, aiming to ensure equal growth and advancement opportunities for all employees. This involves amplifying underrepresented voices, addressing unconscious biases, and providing inclusive, culturally competent mental health care.

Inclusion signifies the practice of granting equal access to opportunities and resources for all employees, particularly those who might otherwise be excluded or marginalized. It ensures that everyone feels a sense of belonging, value, support, and respect as an individual.

Belonging reflects the affinity and positive relationships that develop among employees from diverse backgrounds when businesses actively promote diversity, equity, and inclusion in the workplace.

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.

We have always been 100% independent and 100% remote.We are proud to have built a product that our customers love, and we look for people who have enthusiasm and belief in our mission, vision, and values to join our team. We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

We’re looking for a senior product designer to be the first dedicated designer on our product growth team, shaping how creators get set up, see value in their first days, and enjoy a seamless upgrade experience. Kit ships fast, and the product growth team builds the frameworks and systems that connect creators to the value in their plan as the product keeps changing. We need a designer who can balance rapid experimentation with long-term value for our creators.

Product growth is a new team at Kit, formed earlier this year. In the six months since, we’ve found our rhythm and started to move the numbers, but we’re still shaping how we work, how we prioritise and where we focus in the long term. As the team’s designer, you’ll be the design voice for growth, with real influence over how the team works and what it builds.

You'll own the design of the moments that move our funnel: onboarding, activation, free trials, upgrade and upsell flows, pricing and checkout, and billing. You'll work in a team that learns by shipping, through A/B tests and ship-and-measure changes, and you'll use data to decide what to try next.

This role looks beyond the team. You’ll keep a pulse on what other product teams are shipping and how marketing communicates, so that what creators are promised before sign up matches what they find during onboarding. We’re looking for someone who enjoys looking across Kit to see what's changing, then focusing on what it means for their own work.

This is a full-time IC role for a designer who loves connecting design decisions to numbers, moves quickly without sacrificing quality, and is excited about using AI to accelerate outcomes.

We need someone who thinks deeply about user behaviour, interaction design and the product experience, and who is proactive about finding the next opportunity rather than waiting for a spec.

You'll report to Dan Winer, Director of Product Design, and join a small, high-impact product design team currently consisting of four senior product designers and a senior design engineer. From day one, you’ll be part of the product growth team’s product trio alongside the product manager and engineering manager, shaping the team’s vision, priorities and how we work. You’ll also work closely with the team’s engineers.

You’ll work closely with product marketing on how we talk to creators before and after they sign up, and with the other product designers to stay ahead of what’s shipping across Kit. You’ll also partner with data and our creator success teams.

First week: Get to know Kit’s product and the product growth team’s goals. Get access to Figma, Linear, Mixpanel and our other reporting tools. Take part in get-to-know-you calls with the team.

First month: Get up to speed on how we track and measure creator behaviour in Mixpanel, Omni and Clarity, which experiments are running now, and what we’ve learnt from past ones. Join the product trio’s planning and prioritisation discussions, and ship a small experiment or two, with support from the Director of Product Design as you take ownership of the growth work.

First six months: Own design across the team’s work, which spans growth (onboarding, activation, trials and upgrades) and billing (checkout, plan changes, downgrades and cancellation). This might include personalising onboarding, improving free-to-paid conversion or simplifying our payment flows. By six months, the team should rely on you for a clear picture of how creators move through these flows, where they get stuck and why. You’ll help the team run more and better experiments, from forming a hypothesis to reading the results. We don’t have dedicated researchers, so you’ll talk to creators, run usability tests, track the impact of your work and proactively suggest iterations.

First year: Help the team improve our activation, free-to-paid, trial conversion, and early retention rates, so that more creators upgrade, stay on Kit, and find real value. Turn our upsell, discount and plan change moments into one coherent experience instead of a set of one-off prompts.

Interaction design for complex web applications, with a strong eye for visual hierarchy and detail.

Grounding design decisions in the creator’s voice through interviews, usability testing and customer feedback, partnering with the PM so it shapes what we build as well as how.

Designing flows that change depending on who’s seeing them, like free, trial and paid accounts, or creators upgrading and downgrading.

Designing onboarding, activation, upgrade, pricing, checkout or billing flows.

Experiment design and reading results, including knowing when an A/B test is the right approach or when to just ship and track.

Defining what events to track so the team can measure what you shipped, using tools like Mixpanel.

Clear written and visual communication of design rationale, including async walkthroughs.

Around 5+ years as a product designer on SaaS or other complex web-based products.

Led design on growth, monetisation or onboarding work and can point to its impact on metrics, ideally including retention or quality of conversion, not just volume.

Worked in a cross-functional team with a product manager and engineers, shipping continuously.

Worked within and contributed to an established design system.

A portfolio that shows how research or data informed your decisions and what happened as a result.

Bonus: designed pricing, billing or plan change experiences.

Curious about why users behave the way they do, and about what the numbers aren’t telling you.

Comfortable being wrong in public and sharing what you don’t know, not just what you do.

Proactive and self-directed, with a bias for shipping.

Excited about AI and pragmatic about how to use it.

Low ego and comfortable giving and receiving direct feedback.

Preferring to execute designs handed down from a product manager.

Treating a conversion win as the finish line, without checking whether those creators stay with Kit and find value.

Wanting long, highly-refined projects with little contact with data or results.

Discomfort with experiments that fail or with changing direction based on what we learn.

Focusing on visual polish without deeper thinking about user behaviour and business outcomes.

Being philosophically opposed to using AI in your work.

Needing well-established processes and a fully defined roadmap from day one.

How do we define Diversity, Equity, Inclusion, and Belonging at Talkspace?

Diversity encompasses the unique attributes of our employees as individuals. We value and embrace the richness arising from their varied backgrounds, perspectives, and experiences, which include, but are not limited to, age, ability, ethnicity, gender, race, and cultural background.

Equity refers to a fair and impartial workplace, aiming to ensure equal growth and advancement opportunities for all employees. This involves amplifying underrepresented voices, addressing unconscious biases, and providing inclusive, culturally competent mental health care.

Inclusion signifies the practice of granting equal access to opportunities and resources for all employees, particularly those who might otherwise be excluded or marginalized. It ensures that everyone feels a sense of belonging, value, support, and respect as an individual.

Belonging reflects the affinity and positive relationships that develop among employees from diverse backgrounds when businesses actively promote diversity, equity, and inclusion in the workplace.

As an AI Forward Deployed ProcessOS is a new kind of Camunda product: business processes described in natural language, generated as executable BPMN workflows, and improved autonomously from production data. We’re hiring the first cohort of Forward Deployed Engineers to bring it into production at enterprise customers. You’ll own the deployment journey from first process file to a working, improving system, and hand off a functioning capability to the customer’s team. The code you write and the patterns you discover feed directly back into how ProcessOS is built. This is a rare opportunity to shape a product, a function, and a field practice from day one.

You’ll work end‑to‑end across Java services and React interfaces, collaborating closely with a talented global team to measure, improve, and scale performance and fault tolerance in real-world distributed systems.

Deploy and prove. Own the journey from first process file to production workflow. At 90 days: at least one workflow running, the improvement loop active, and measurable fitness gains the customer can see. At 12 months: 3–5 customers with viable deployments.

Drive organizational adoption. Work across technical and business stakeholders to build understanding and trust, turning early deployments into lasting customer capability.

Work at depth technically. Configure and run Camunda clusters locally and in cloud environments. Debug integration failures. Build custom agents where platform defaults don’t fit. Write connector logic that gets legacy enterprise systems talking to ProcessOS.

Build the playbook. There is no deployment template yet. Document what good looks like as you discover it, and make the next implementation faster.

Feed the product. Custom integrations you write get contributed back. Integration patterns get codified. Production bugs you find, you often fix. Your field work is a direct input to ProcessOS.

Hand off deliberately. Leave customers with a team that can operate independently, is trained on the platform, and acts on improvement results without you.

Enterprise production experience: you’ve shipped something inside a large organization and stayed to see it operate. You know why governance and resilience matter at scale.

Technical depth: You can debug an integration failure, write a custom agent against a legacy API, stand up a Camunda cluster from scratch, and explain generated BPMN to both engineers and business leads.

#7116. Lifecycle QA Specialist at Winona

Winona is one of the leading telemedicine companies providing HRT for women in menopause. We’ve built all of our technology in-house including an EHR, patient care admin system, patient portal, website, marketing technology etc. Winona has two compounding pharmacies and a team of in-house physicians providing world-class care via a fully vertically integrated business model.

We are seeking a detail-oriented QA Specialist to ensure that every email and SMS campaign and journey is configured correctly and functions as intended prior to launch. This role spans campaign configuration and tracking, content and design review, and cross-platform QA — identifying and resolving issues before they reach our audience.

Conduct QA on all email and SMS campaigns and journeys in Iterable prior to launch to ensure every element is configured correctly and functions as intended.

Verify campaign and journey names against Winona’s naming conventions (name, year, event, medium and audience).

Review journey configuration, including start tiles, suppressions, scheduled send dates and times, tile logic and conditionals, entry limits, exit rules and journey event tracking.

Confirm that monthly and recurring journeys are refreshed with updated start tiles prior to each send.

Verify that campaign labels are applied correctly (audience, segments, category, goal and month/year).

Confirm that the appropriate conversion events are assigned for each campaign type: prospect, customer upsell, Shop, community and live event campaigns.

Review delivery settings, including quiet hours, send time optimization, frequency caps and performance alerts.

Verify that link tracking is configured correctly, including Google Analytics, UTM parameters and custom link parameters.

Review sender details: message type, from name, from email and reply-to address.

Review email templates against Winona’s brand and design standards, including fonts, sizing, padding, CTA button styling, link formatting and footers.

Test all links, CTA buttons and image links, and confirm that images include alt text.

Proofread copy for accuracy, consistency and formatting.

Send and review test proofs in light and dark mode on iOS and Android, across both Gmail and Apple Mail.

Coordinate SMS test sends with the appropriate team members prior to testing.

Conduct QA on email touchpoints that connect to Shopify, such as Shop campaigns and checkout-related flows, to ensure a seamless end-to-end experience.

Follow and help maintain internal templating documentation, QA checklists and the HTML template library.

Communicate issues, errors and inconsistencies to the lifecycle team clearly and promptly, and track them through resolution.

Recommend improvements to QA processes and standards on an ongoing basis.

At least 3 years of experience performing QA within an ESP like Iterable or similar platforms.

Working knowledge of HTML and CSS for email.

Exceptional attention to detail and consistent checklist execution.

Understanding of how emails render across clients, devices and dark mode.

Clear written communication for reporting issues.

Proficiency with Handlebars or dynamic content personalization.

Shopify experience, particularly with checkout and order flows.

Experience with email testing tools such as Litmus or Email on Acid.

Familiarity with Google Analytics and UTM tracking.

Experience with ClickUp or similar project management tools.

Knowledge of SMS best practices and compliance standards (CAN-SPAM, CASL).

As a full-time independent contractor, you’ll enjoy:

Paid non-working holidays per country of residence

#7099. How Richmond, Virginia, schools are tackling a $680M facilities funding shortfall

Let K-12 Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Voters approved a 1% sales tax to help with funding. In an assessment, each school’s needs are weighed against what replacement would cost.

As it became clear in early November that Richmond voters would pass a 1% sales tax measure for school construction and renovation, Richmond Public Schools Superintendent Jason Kamras was grateful that residents would approve a tax increase even while households around the country are experiencing an affordability crisis.

“We are dealing with decades of deferred maintenance [and] disinvestment,” Kamras told Facilities Dive. “Our challenges are legion, which is why I think the voters so overwhelmingly supported the 1%.”

The district faces a roughly $680 million, 20-year backlog in maintenance, repair and upgrades to a portfolio of more than 40 schools, according to an independent assessment the agency conducted in 2024. Almost a quarter of the schools are more than 100 years old. About 70% are more than 50 years old. Only a handful are less than 15 years old.

Earlier this month, voters in Richmond were asked to decide whether to increase the sales tax by 1% to create a fund exclusively to build new public schools and renovate existing facilities. When the final votes were counted, 68% of the electorate voted in favor of the assessment, according to an Axios Richmond report.

Kamras says the actual need is even more than what the assessment shows — “well north of $1 billion,” he said.

With passage of the 1% assessment, the district could raise an estimated $850 million over the next 20 years. But that’s still not enough to meet all of the needs, Kamras said.

“I wish the sales tax would solve everything,” he said. “It's going to take us a lot farther, a lot faster, but it by itself does not solve all of our facilities' needs.”

The district currently gets about $2.5 million a year for facilities work, according to the school.

Adding to its challenges, the district fired its director of facilities earlier this year for improper use of funds and as of mid-November was operating with an interim director, according to the district’s website. The former facilities chief is alleged to have made personal purchases using his school district credit card, according to news reports.

“The investigation found direct evidence of asset misappropriation and unprofessional conduct” by the official, a school district investigation found, ABC 8 News reported in October.

Among the needs the district faces is a $938,000 roof repair at Woodville Elementary School, $2.5 million for brick wall repairs at John Marshall High School and $1.7 million for updated windows at Thomas Jefferson High School.

Mold, flood damage and HVAC issues are widespread, according to the assessment, which used a facility condition index to determine what each of the school’s needs are weighed against what it would cost to replace the school.

“The Facility Condition Index … is defined as the ratio of the cost of current needs divided by the current replacement value (CRV) of the facility,” the assessment says.

Schools that score between 0 and 5 are considered new or well-maintained, with little or no visual evidence of wear or deficiencies. Those with a score of between 5 and 10 show wear but are still in a serviceable and functioning condition. Those with a score of between 10 and 30 show signs of hard or long-term wear and are nearing the end of their useful or serviceable life. Those with a score of 30 and above have reached the end of their useful or serviceable life, making “renewal” necessary.

The Richmond City Council will need to pass an ordinance to enshrine the sales tax increase into law. The district has been using funds from a local meals tax to patch together funds for school construction dating back to 2018.

Kamras said the city will determine which projects to prioritize based on feedback from nine stakeholder meetings, as well as building conditions, enrollment trends, and concentration of student need.

Richmond is not alone in its need for school renovations. School districts on average only receive about 17% of their construction and equipment funding from states, according to 21st Century School Fund’s State of our Schools report. The federal government doesn’t help much, either, adding just 3.2% of the funds schools need, the report says. Local communities tend to be left to foot the bill.

The result is that U.S. public schools face an $85 billion shortfall on facilities, the report found.

Mary Filardo, executive director of the 21st Century School Fund, would like to see the states and federal government do more to support school facilities across the country, she told Facilities Dive. She said the country should view its ailing school infrastructure similar to debt.

“You can not replace that leaky roof, but it's not going away. You still owe that building a roof,” she said.

Filardo said that schools’ HVAC systems are complicated. They’re not just for temperature control, but also air filtration and dehumidification. Retrofitting older schools also presents unique challenges.

“In the 1950s, there would be one outlet in a classroom, maybe,” she said. “If you were lucky, one on each wall.”

From the states’ perspectives, Filardo sees a wide range of approaches to start addressing needs. Wyoming, for example, has used coal lease bonuses — mining company payments for extraction permits — and Massachusetts has implemented a statewide sales tax.

Meanwhile, in Virginia, Richmond was one of many seeking to boost facilities budgets. The same measure failed to pass in 32 of the 47 localities statewide.

Kristen Nye, executive director of Virginians for Modern Schools, an organization supporting the measure, said voter education proved to be very important in Richmond’s case. She added that the measure opened up opportunities that may have previously seemed out of reach.

“Let's say those plans haven't been touched in well over a decade,” Nye said. “They're sort of blowing the dust off of them and taking them back out in the community again.”

Richmond City Councilor Katherine Jordan led the city’s legislation that put the sales tax measure on the ballot.

“Very often education is a popular talking point on the campaign trail, but when it comes to budget time, somehow it just doesn't make it through,” she said.

Richmond School Board Member Katie Ricard said that if the measure had not passed, it might have been put back on the ballot for a future year. RPS might also have looked to the state for more funding.

Although the measure passed, the school district will need additional funding, said Ricard, noting that she expects the district to continue applying for grants.

“We have 42 school buildings across the district,” Ricard said. “It just gets really costly to maintain all of them. They are old, and their systems are pretty much hanging on by a paperclip, sometimes until they fail.”

#7093. 87% of educators use personal devices in teaching. The next great 1:1 investment might be the one at the front of the room.

Even in districts that issue laptops to staff, there is a good chance a teacher’s personal laptop, tablet or phone is carrying some of the workload. In fact, a recent study by the University of Arizona found that 87% of educators surveyed use their personal devices for at least some teaching-related tasks. Those personal devices were purchased, set up and managed by themselves, with two-thirds reporting that they download institutional documents (for example student data, grade books) onto them.

While districts are providing teaching devices, these numbers suggest that alongside the school-issued laptop, a share of teaching work, from grade books to student records, ends up on devices the district cannot see or manage.

In the movement around enabling technology in classrooms for students, this study presents a larger conversation on how technology is being used by educators who are working closest to them.

Over the last two decades, classroom devices have gone from shared computer labs to one-to-one deployment. Today, 96% of teachers say their students use learning devices in class. This did not happen overnight. It took years of planning from administrators, careful execution by IT teams, significant investment and district grants. As a result, student devices have a tried-and-true playbook. Districts know what to expect with refresh cycles, IT teams understand device management and superintendents can anticipate their budgets and funding for new devices.

Teachers are the cornerstone of classroom tech, and it is time their devices got a playbook too. As they regularly carry heavy computing loads, in everything from running lessons, online grading, to joining calls, they deserve the same level of detail and detail to device deployment and management. This is more handing out a laptop. It means giving teachers a device capable enough that they don’t need to reach for their own.

This is even more important as teachers regularly handle sensitive data. As the study found, more than half of participants who downloaded institutional documents onto personal devices, reported that their devices had automatic cloud backups through personal accounts.

While using a personal device for teaching might seem harmless in nature, the device is not under the districts' scope. Updates, encryption and sign-in guidelines are not under the control of the district, rather, this responsibility falls to the teacher.

This is alarming as education remains the fourth most targeted sector for cyber-attacks. Robust student, teacher and parent data combined with increased digitalization and lack of cybersecurity resources have left devices and information vulnerable to malicious attacks.

Paired with the right training on cybersecurity best-practices, a well-managed district-deployed device overseen by IT teams helps to enable schools to secure sensitive student data. These devices deploy continuous updates, encourage fast and secure sign-in and ensure private information stays secure.

The ASUS P5 featuring the Intel® Core™ Ultra 7 Series 3 Processor, is a strong example of a device that can be managed at scale with the performance to multitask through a full teaching day. Engineered to seamlessly juggle grade books, lesson plans and video calls, Intel processors deliver all-day battery life and built-in hardware-based security that helps protect student data — that’s the power of Intel Inside®. For IT teams, the device supports centralized management tools that let them deploy updates, monitor device health and enforce policies across a district’s fleet.

The planning, budgeting and IT expertise that has put a device in nearly every student’s hands in the United States is ready to extend to teachers. With the right devices, they can do even more and districts can protect the data that matters most. The next great 1:1 investment might be the one at the front of the room.

#7090. Building an executive business case for unified college and career readiness infrastructure

As states and school districts deepen their commitment to college and career readiness, there is increased focus on accountability. Complex state graduation requirements, workforce readiness indicators and reporting mandates increasingly require districts to provide evidence that proves their students are actually progressing toward post-secondary success.

While accountability matters, practitioners understand that policies and metrics alone do not move the needle. Educators need the bandwidth and quality instructional programming to guide learners across their K–12 journeys. Students themselves need authentic, engaging opportunities to explore pathways, connect with caring adults and take ownership of their futures.

Meeting these expectations demands a coherent system that aligns central district priorities, educator capacity and day-to-day student experiences. Building the executive case for that shift requires leaders to demonstrate how unified CCR infrastructure can deliver a meaningful return.

In many states across the country, districts are required to demonstrate that students are completing required readiness activities, updating graduation plans and meeting statutory benchmarks across every campus. Managing each requirement—from career readiness indicators to financial literacy and work-based learning—as an isolated program creates administrative friction and disparate student records that need to be reconciled for reporting.

Adopting a comprehensive system changes that dynamic. In a national study conducted by Hanover Research, 84% of surveyed district leaders using a unified platform reported that it successfully supports multiple district requirements and core workflows. An identical 84% reported an enhanced capability to fulfill reporting requirements, while 70% cited a reduction in time spent on manual administrative tasks like logging lessons and tracking transcripts.

A coordinated strategy delivered through a centralized platform gives district leaders the infrastructure to satisfy complex statutory demands without juggling separate data sources and disjointed spreadsheets.

Centralizing and simplifying workflows reduces administrative burden, giving educators additional bandwidth for personalized student guidance.

A cohesive and collaborative district-wide readiness strategy relies on educators having easy, reliable visibility into student progress. When tools and data operate in silos, counselors and teachers spend precious advising time tracking down files or working with fragmented information, rather than working directly with students.

A connected system reclaims that time. The Hanover study found that educators using a unified platform experienced an estimated 33% reduction in administrative workloads tied to readiness workflows. Crucially, 54% reported improved efficiency in student advising. When routine clerical tasks are automated, counselors enter conversations with multi-year context in hand, turning transactional compliance check-ins into meaningful, high-value guidance.

Connecting workflows cuts down operational drag and restores educator capacity for direct student mentorship.

District investments deliver the most meaningful returns when they nurture self-directed learners, rather than usher students through rote tasks to complete checklists.

When planning is embedded as a continuous developmental journey, instead of an 11th-hour graduation requirement, student engagement deepens. Among educators and district leaders surveyed by Hanover with a unified platform in place, 76% reported greater clarity in students’ post-secondary goals, 74% observed increased confidence in post-secondary planning, and 73% cited stronger student ownership over the entire process.

Giving educators a shared, full K-12, cross-department platform creates an unbroken continuum of support, encouraging students to explore options, recognize their strengths and actively shape their life after high school.

Most districts already invest in a variety of programs to address compliance, staff needs and career exploration. Breakdowns occur because these efforts operate in isolated silos.

Separate CCR initiatives and tools creates redundant work for staff, disconnected data erases momentum during middle-to-high-school transitions, and fragmented reporting blinds district leadership to participation gaps across buildings.

Delivering a unified district-wide CCR program requires education, communication, collaboration and planning. A unified infrastructure helps enable a coherent program by connecting existing efforts, so every localized investment contributes to a larger strategic framework.

Unified infrastructure doesn’t replace the work districts are already doing. It connects that work so each investment contributes to a broader strategy.

Building an executive business case requires evaluating readiness systems beyond traditional accounting metrics. The Hanover report establishes a Return on Impact framework that measures value across four interconnected dimensions:

Hanover’s findings reinforce the organizational value of a unified system: 74% of respondents reported stronger accountability across grade spans, while 73% pointed to enhanced support for strategic planning and data-informed decision-making.

To translate these findings into practical leadership discussions, district decision makers can anchor evaluations around four questions:

District leaders do not have to choose between operational efficiency, state compliance and student success; a well-designed CCR strategy advances all three. Sustainable readiness infrastructure centers on what a school community can achieve when compliance mandates, staff capacity and student exploration are linked through one coordinated strategy.

To learn how a unified platform transforms CCR strategy into long-term impact, visit Xello.world.

#7080. Texas Democrats propose bills to nix state DEI ban at public colleges

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The party clinched the state’s House and governorship in the midterm elections, though that still may not be enough to get the legislation passed.

In the state’s House, Reps. Nicole Collier and John Bucy III have put forward bills that would repeal the 2023 law. Texas state Sen. José Menéndez introduced a companion bill in the state’s Senate.

At any other time in the past three decades, the new bills might have faced little chance of passage simply by virtue of being championed by Democrats. But the party now controls two-thirds of a trifecta for the first time since 1996 — a makeup that may still not be enough to pass the legislation.

Senate Bill 17 — the target of the new bills — bans several DEI activities at public colleges, including:

Texas was one of the first states to ban DEI offices and initiatives in higher education. Following SB 17 and a similar law in Florida, universities from Ohio to Iowa to Alaska shuttered their DEI offices, often under legislative mandates.

Institutions quickly responded to SB 17. For instance, the University of North Texas said before the law took effect that it would restructure its Multicultural Center and Pride Alliance into the student affairs office and launch a new Center for Belonging and Engagement.

After passage, the Texas A&M University System issued guidance stating that DEI terminology couldn’t be used on the institution’s websites. Offices dedicated to student affinity communities would have to close, though it said some programs and libraries could be absorbed into broader offices and initiatives.

Texas Tech University also closed its DEI office.

Texas Tech University System’s latest chancellor — Brandon Creighton,  who helped author SB 17 — has gone even further than the bill’s requirements, which don’t cover academic content. The system has moved to eliminate academic programs focused on sexual orientation and gender identity and put restrictions on teaching about race, sex and gender in courses.

State audits so far have determined that the University of Texas System, nearly all of the institutions within theTexas A&M University System and 19 of the state’s community colleges are compliant with SB 17.

Audits last year deemed two institutions noncompliant — a community college because it made a new employee participate in a DEI training and Texas A&M-Central Texas because it struck a contract that didn’t conform to the law’s ban.

#7077. VP Sales, UK & Europe at Halcyon

#7076. School specialist’s time spent on IEPs after hours may count toward FMLA eligibility

Let K-12 Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The finding comes in a recent U.S. Department of Labor opinion letter involving a salaried occupational therapist.

DOL’s Nov. 17 FMLA opinion letter could affect how school districts and other employers determine whether an employee is eligible for leave.

Generally, employees are eligible for leave for a serious health condition or other covered reason if they work for an employer that is subject to the law for at least 12 months, have at least 1,250 hours of service with the employer during the 12 months before their FMLA leave starts, and work at a location where the employer has at least 50 employees within 75 miles.

Without accurate timekeeping, it’s up to the employer to demonstrate that an employee does not qualify for FMLA leave, DOL said. And if that lack of recordkeeping is because the employee is exempt from the Fair Labor Standards Act’s recordkeeping requirements for minimum wage and overtime purposes, “eligibility for FMLA leave is presumed for any employee who has been employed for at least 12 months,” according to FMLA regulations.

FMLA requires employers to provide up to 12 workweeks of unpaid, job-protected leave annually for qualifying family and medical reasons.

For the occupational therapist in question, DOL said, their after-hours tasks must be counted as hours of service. And while the agency said it didn’t have enough information to determine that the individual’s travel time must definitively count toward their service hours, it appeared likely to be the case.
